Third Annual Report of the Director of Insurance as of December 31, 1955.

PRINCIPAL ACCOMPLISHMENTS OF THE INSURANCE DEPARTMENT in 1955 

Collected more than $2, 000, 000 in premium taxes, fees and licenses for the State Treasurer. 

Administered the provisions of the Arizona Insurance Code of 1955 Passed upon qualifications of insurers applying for admission to do business in Arizona. 

Examined the financial statements and premium tax returns of all insurers doing business in Arizona and the affairs, transactions, accounts, records and assets of domestic insurers. 

Inspected and filed about 5, 000 affidavits for surplus line placements. 

Reviewed about 8, 400 policies and about 7,500 fire and casualty rate filings submitted for approval for use in Arizona.

Investigated and serviced about 2,500 policyholder's complaints and answered numerous inquiries from the general public. 

Processed about 50, 000 insurer's appointments of agents and about 10, 500 agent's, broker's, solicitor's, and miscellaneous licenses. 

Prepared, supervised and graded about 2, 200 written examinations of applicants for agent's, broker's and solicitor's licenses. 

Responded to the steady inflow of correspondence, questionnaires and requests pertaining to insurance. 79150

EXPENSES OF THE INSURANCE DEPARTMENT

Director. To that amount was added $16, 720.00 for the purpose of providing the Insurance Department with adequate office facilities and the necessary equipment which made available the sum of $85, 184.00 for all functions of the department.

World Wide Brotherhood, Phoenix, Arizona (Did not renew)

Zurich General Accident and Liability Insurance Company, Chicago, Illinois (Name changed to Zurich Insurance Company) Trans-Pacific Insurance Company 

On September 7, 1955, at the request of the Board of Directors of Trans-Pacific Insurance Company, an automobile casualty insurance company, the Director of Insurance applied to the Maricopa County Superior Court for and secured an order directing him to take over the affairs of the company for the purpose of rehabilitation. While an association examination of the affairs of Trans-Pacific conducted by the Arizona Insurance Department in cooperation with the Insurance Department of the States of South Carolina and Indiana had indicated that the company was in a solvent position as of December 31, 1954, adverse loss experience and an unsatisfactory reinsurance situation had progressively weakened the company's condition during the spring and summer of 1955. Upon being appointed as receiver of the company for the purpose of rehabilitation, the 

Director of Insurance caused an immediate survey to be made of the condition of the company and determined that rehabilitation was not feasible. Therefore on September 15, 1955, the Director applied to the Maricopa County Superior Court for and secured an order directing him to proceed to liquidate the company. Liquidation has proceeded in an orderly manner with the adjudication of contested claims and the institution of litigation in various parts of the United States for the purpose of marshaling and collecting assets.

OPINIONS OF THE DEPARTMENT OF LAW OF CONTINUING IN PORTANCE RELATING TO THE TRANSACTION AND REGULATION OF INSURANCE 

Robert Morrison, Attorney General 

February 18, 1955

Opinion No. 55-27 

The Department was advised that a person exercising the rights and powers and undertaking the duties and obligations in Arizona of a managing general agent "shall in Arizona act" within the meaning of R. S. A. Section 20238(A) and, therefore, must be licensed even though all functions of such person are performed or directed entirely from outside the territorial boundaries of Arizona. 

February 28, 1955

Opinion No. 55-36 

The Department was advised that the qualification requirement in R.S. A. Section 20-289 of six months residency prior to application for license as agent or broker must be fully complied with before application for temporary license under R.S. A. Section 20-307(A)7 will be considered, since the evident purpose of the latter Section is to accommodate only those applicants, then engaged in a bona fide study of insurance, who are in all respects qualified except as to passing a written examination. 

March 22, 1955

Opinion No. 55-58 

The Securities Division of the Corporation Commission was advised that within the meaning of R. S. A. Sections 

20-153 and 20-454 a domestic life insurer is "engaged" in a program wherein policyholders are granted the right to buy securities in domestic corporations only if transactions are being conducted with some frequency and continuity so as to occupy the time, attention and labor of individuals seeking a livelihood or profit. 

March 22, 1955

Opinion No. 55-60 

The Department was advised that it would not be contrary to the policy loan requirements in R.S. A. Section 201209 or the Standard Nonforfeiture Law in R. S. A. Section 20-1231 for a life insurance policy to contain a provision (1) adding "automatic premium loan" to the policyholder's options in event of default in any premium payment or (2) making "automatic premium loan" the stipulated alternative should the policyholder fail to indicate preference among nonforfeiture options.

April 1, 1955 

Opinion No. 55-75 

The Department was advised that R.S.A. Section 20-1563(A)3, which increased the statutory deposit requirement of title insurers from $50,000 to $100, 000, applies to those insurers holding a Certificate of Authority since prior to January 1, 1955 as well as to those holding such Certificate only subsequent to said date but that R. S. A. Section 20214 excused until January 1, 1956 the first-mentioned insurers from compliance with said increased requirement. 

April 25, 1955

Opinion No. 55-90 

The Department was advised (1) that R. S. A. Section 20-156 by inference empowers the Director of Insurance to compel an insurer to furnish him with such premium history records or interpretations thereof as he might demand in behalf of a complaining policyholder and (2) that R.S. A. Section 20-158(D) renders the decision concerning release or disclosure of examinations or investigation reports entirely within the administrative discretion of the Director of 

Insurance. 

April 28, 1955

Opinion No. 55-95 

The Department was advised that R.S. A. Section 20-292(C) requires an applicant for agent's license to be examined for all kinds of insurance authorized to be transacted in Arizona by the insurer or insurers proposed to be represented, even though he has been granted no authority or appointment to represent such insurer with respect to one or more kinds of such insurance and even though his license, if issued, is to be limited to less than all kinds of insurance so authorized to be transacted. 

April 28, 1955

Opinion No. 55-96 

The Department was advised that, in the event of a creditor's sale or transfer to another party of an indebtedness or mortgage, R.S. A. Section 20-1253, specifying requirements for debtor group life insurance, would not of itself compel a termination of group insurance previously contracted by the creditor on the lives of his debtors. 10

June 7, 1955

Opinion No. 55-100 

The Department was advised that, even though R.S. A. Section 20-1252(1) rendered a corporate director ineligible for group employee life coverage, nevertheless an insurer in 1955 could issue a life policy to an employee group including within it a corporate director, because R.S. A. Section 20-111 granted a grace period of one year to bring certain existing practices into conformity with the requirements and provisions of the new Insurance Code, effective January 1, 1955. 

May 11, 1955

Opinion No. 55-107 

The Department was advised that an undertaking to supply needed ambulance service without additional cost to those prepaying a $12 annual fee therefor is an agreement to pay for contingent services rather than to indemnify for expenses incurred in ambulance hire and, therefore, is a service contract and not insurance as defined in R. S. A. 

Section 20-103. 

May 26, 1955

Opinion No. 55-133 

The Department was advised that R.S. A. Section 20-1255(3), specifying requirements for trustee group life insurance, prohibits an insurer from terminating employee coverage solely on the grounds that the employer has discontinued membership in an association of employers, even though the existence of such membership had been a condition precedent to placement of the insurance and even though the prohibition might invite abuse from unscrupulous employers or, through no fault of its own, increase costs to the insurer. 

May 31, 1955

Opinion No. 55-136 

The Securities Division of the Corporation Commission was advised that a "survivors' bonus fund" or similar certificate attached to and declared to be part of a life insurance policy and creating a trust fund of dividends to be invested by the insurer in a group list of stocks and stipulating that principal and interest are to be distributed only to surviving participants not in default -- is within the meaning of R. S. A. Section 44-1801(13) a "certificate of interest in a trust", is therefore a "security" and is not exempt from registration under R.S. A. Section 44-1843. 

June 16, 1955

Opinion No. 55-148 

The Department was advised that under R. S. A. Section 20-363, pertaining to services of rating organizations, an insurer, while maintaining a subscribership in a licensed fire rating bureau, may file independently for classes of insurance normally under the jurisdiction of such a bureau, but that under R. S. A. Section 20-349 an insurer instead of maintaining a subscribership in such a bureau may file its own rates for fire insurance if maintaining what the Director of Insurance in his discretion considers to be adequate rating facilities of its own. 

July 28, 1955

Opinion No. 55-166 

The Department was advised that under R. S. A. Section 20-342(D) multiple line insurers may market a package policy with indivisible premium combining fire and casualty coverages but that under R.S. A. Section 9-951 such portion of premium fairly attributable to fire insurance would be subject to the Firemen's Relief and Pension Fund assessment. 

December 6, 1955

Opinion No. 56-4 

The State Auditor was advised that with respect to insurance the Director of Insurance and not the Corporation Commission is the budget unit head with authority to file allotment requests, to receive and spend appropriation money and to approve claims for payment, because R.S. A., Title 20, Chapter 1, Article 2 made the Insurance Department a separate and distinct administrative body. 

April 25, 1956

Opinion No. 56-89 

The Department was advised that a mutual life insurer converted from a fraternal benefit society need not pay the 2% net premiums tax, imposed upon foreign insurers by R. S. A. Section 20-224 (B), on renewal assessments received in maintenance of fraternal business placed prior to conversion, because R. S. A. Section 20-890 exempts from taxation fraternal business even of an insurer which also engages in taxable transactions. 

July 3, 1956

Opinion No. 56-119 

The Department was advised that an insurer, domiciled in a state which would impose upon Arizona insurers a net premiums tax in excess of 2% on business transacted, in calculating the retaliatory amount due under R.S. A. Section 

20-230(A) may deduct from sums otherwise payable the "aggregate" or totality of licenses and fees paid to Arizona, in behalf of itself and on account of its transactions and operations, in excess of those licenses and fees which the state of its domicile would have required from identical transactions and operations in said state by an Arizona insurer, because the sole function of a retaliatory statute is to equalize and minimize burdens between states. 11
